8. Power Supplies

   Power Supply PP-846/U (fig. 6) is used to
supply filament, plate, and bias voltages to
Radio Receiver R-257/U in Radio Receiving
Set AN/FRR-44. The power supply chassis
mounts in a vertical plane and is fastened to
the receiver base with six screws. This power
supply operates on 115 or 230 volts a-c (alter-
nating current). For further details on Power
Supply PP-846/U, see TM 11-5078. Power
Supplies PP-867/U, PP—868/U, and PP-869/U
(TM 11-5079), which also can be used to power
the receiver, are similar to Power Supply

superheterodyne f-m receiver. It is designed       is coupled to first mixer V203 together with a

                                                            70. Checking Filament Circuits

                                                                a. Radio Receiver R-—257/U uses two fila-
                                                            ment voltages, 6.3-volt a-c or d-c, and 1.4-volt
                                                            d-c. Each plug-in unit (except the RELAY
                                                            plug-in unit) contains an interlock jumper
                                                            which opens the 1.4-volt filament circuit when
                                                            the plug-in unit is removed. The normal fila-
                                                            ment voltage depends on all the tubes drawing
                                                            their normal current. If it were not for this
                                                            filament interlock feature, the filament voltage
                                                            on the remaining tubes would rise whenever
                                                            one of the plug-in units was removed from the
                                                            receiver base.

                                                                b. Reference to figure 45 shows the filament
                                                            distribution through the receiver. Ground con-
                                                            nections marked GND 1 through GND 4 are
                                                             specific ground points in the receiver base, and
                                                             are indicated as such for the purpose of loca-
                                                            tion and _ identification. This schematic dia-
                                                             gram aids in locating defects in the filament
                                                             circuits.

                                                                                                                                      51


---

   c. Most of the filaments are bypassed to
ground with ceramic capacitors. Defects in
the filament circuits may be caused by shorts
through these capacitors.

71. Checking B+ and Filament Circuit

Defects in the Receiver Base

   a. The receiver base consists mainly of cables
and connectors with the exception of a few
resistors and capacitors. If a defect in the
B+ circuit or the filament circuit has been sec-
tionalized to the receiver base, check the base
as described below.

   b. Pull the power cord plug from the source
of power. Remove the receiver from its case

and also remove the power supply and all the
plug-in units. The receiver base schematic

diagram (fig. 37) and the overall receiver sche-
matics (fig. 79) will be of assistance in trouble-
shooting. If the trouble is known to be in the

filament circuits, use the filament circuits sche-  NOTES:
matic diagram (fig. 45). If the trouble lies
in the B+ circuits, use the B+ distribution

diagram (fig. 44).                                  |. RESISTANCE MEASUREMENTS MADE   2. ALL RESISTANCE MEASUREMENTS

material available and the conditions under            a. Smash. Smash the crystals, controls,
which the equipment is to be shipped or stored.    tubes, coils, switches, capacitors, and trans-

   b. Whenever practicable, place a dehydrat-      crowbars, or other heavy tools.
ing agent, such as silica gel, inside the case or
carton which contains the receiver. Protect            b. Cut. Cut cords and wiring; use axes.
it with a waterproof paper barrier. Seal the       handaxes, or machetes.
seams with sealing compound or tape. Provide
at least 3 inches of excelsior padding between        c. Burn. Burn cords, resistors, capacitors,
the paper barrier and packing case.                coils, wiring, and technical manuals; use gaso-
